# Photo report — every shipped photo, its source, and the judgment

The 2b rule (tightened 2026-08-14): **a clean solo portrait or honest
initials — never a screengrab, banner, logo crop, or a photo where the
person is not the unambiguous sole subject.** This report exists so a
human can verify every photo the product ships without hunting through
data files: each row names the person, the file or URL served, where it
came from, and why it passed. A photo not listed here does not ship —
`tests/candidate-cards.test.ts` asserts every shipped photo URL appears
in this file.

## Round 5 — the doors reopen the challenger sweep (2026-08-15, D50)

**590 of 750 → 592 of 750.** Two portraits shipped, from eleven candidates
and forty-six staged images. That ratio is the finding, and it is a better
outcome than round 4's zero for a reason worth writing down.

### What the author's ruling changed, and what it did not

`CLAUDE.md`'s photo rule was rewritten whole on 2026-08-15 into **four
doors**, with the composition and identity standards **lifted out of the
doors entirely**. Round 4 had walked both of the old rungs to the end for all
76 ballot challengers and shipped nothing, correctly: a campaign website was
neither of the two doors that existed then. Doors 3 and 4 answer exactly that
question —

- **Door 3** — the campaign's OWN self-published portrait from its OWN site,
  labelled "campaign-provided", source recorded, remove-on-request.
- **Door 4**, last resort — any portrait on the candidate's own campaign site
  or on any `.gov` site.

**The standards did not move an inch**, and that is why nine of eleven
candidates still ship initials. A door is permission to use a source; it is
never permission to ship a worse picture.

### The mechanical finding: an og:image is a banner by design

Round 4 staged **one** image per candidate — the `og:image` — and nine of ten
were banners. That was read at the time as "campaign sites publish banners".
It is more precisely a fact about the format: **an `og:image` is a
social-share card, and a share card is designed to be a banner with the
candidate's name across it.** The headshot a campaign puts on its About page
was never being looked at.

`scripts/hunt-campaign-photos.mts` now collects the page's own `<img>`
elements as well, filters the structural non-portraits (logos, icons,
donate buttons, anything under 4 KB) and stages up to six per candidate. That
one change is the whole difference between round 4's zero and round 5's two:
both shipped portraits came from `<img>`, and **neither** was the `og:image`.

### Shipped

| Candidate | Contest | File | Door | Judgment |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Teri Ann Hourihan | AZ Governor | `/candidates/az-pima-governor-hourihan-teri-ann.jpg` | 3 | Clean solo studio portrait, plain grey ground, unambiguous sole subject. The campaign's own file on its own domain, named `Teri+Headshot-Full+Res-11.jpg` — the name is the campaign's, which is also the identity evidence. Source `https://teriann4azgov.org`, read 2026-08-15. |
| Walter "Les" Presmyk | AZ Mine Inspector | `/candidates/az-pima-mine-inspector-presmyk-walter-les.jpg` | 3 | Clean solo studio portrait, plain grey ground, unambiguous sole subject. The campaign's own file, named `Headshot.jpg`. Source `https://lespresmyk.com`, read 2026-08-15. |
| Risa Lombardo | AZ Governor | `/candidates/az-pima-governor-lombardo-risa.png` | 3 | Clean solo studio portrait, plain grey ground, circular crop on transparency, unambiguous sole subject. Published on her own domain's About page as `risal-1024x1024.png`. Source `https://risalombardo.com`, read 2026-08-15. **Shipped 2026-08-15 (item 6b), after the author named her as still faceless on the 85701 ballot.** The earlier miss was recorded honestly and was wrong in scope, not in fact: the hunt had checked only the home page's `og:image`, which is a WORDMARK ("RISA LOMBARDO / GREEN FOR GOVERNOR" over a party emblem) and correctly failed. Door 3 is the campaign's own portrait ANYWHERE on its own site, not only in a meta tag. **For the author's ruling:** the campaign's own treatment places a small party emblem inside the lower-right of the circle. It is a portrait carrying a badge rather than a badge standing in for a portrait, so it passes here — recorded so the call is yours to reverse. PNG with transparency, so it ships as `.png`; the stripper found no metadata to remove and said so. |

Both were fetched once, EXIF-stripped (`APP1` removed from each, verified),
committed under `public/candidates/` and served from our own origin. Both
cards carry `campaign-provided … Removed on request.`

**A retrieval detail that would have shipped an unstripped file**:
Squarespace's CDN content-negotiates, and handed back a **WebP** for a `.jpg`
URL. The metadata stripper only understands JPEG and PNG and correctly
refused rather than shipping bytes it could not inspect — so the fetch now
sends `Accept: image/jpeg,image/png` explicitly. The guard caught this, not a
person.

### Not shipped, per candidate, with the reason

Every one of these was hunted with the same ladder walked. Doors 1 and 2 had already
failed for all of them in earlier rounds (no Commons licence, no government
body publishes a portrait of a challenger); doors 3 and 4 were walked here.

| Candidate | Contest | Staged | Why nothing shipped |
|---|---|---|---|

The one photograph is a clean solo portrait but its filename is Facebook's CDN pattern (`710724279_1016778710694891_…_n-1.jpg`) — **social-media images are prohibited at every door**, and re-hosting one on the campaign's own server does not change what it is. Recorded rather than shipped. |
| JoAnna Mendoza | AZ CD-6 | 3 | All three are rally photographs with other people plainly visible; the candidate is not the unambiguous sole subject. |

The two portraits are Canva compositions named `Untitled design.png` and `Untitled design-2.png`, carrying no name evidence at all. Any doubt about identity fails at every door, and this is doubt. |
| Joe Hathaway | NJ CD-11 | 3 | The og:image is a wordmark-plus-portrait banner. One solo outdoor photograph and one family group; neither filename (`8599_500.jpg`, `8599_156.jpg`) carries identity evidence, and the family group contains a similar-looking man. Doubt again. |
| Rebecca Harned | NH Senate 7 | 6 | Two wordmarks, a woman with two dogs with the face partly obscured, a two-person canoe photograph, and a firefighters' association logo. |

### The 65 challengers with no recorded campaign site

The eleven above are the challengers whose campaign site is recorded in
`data/ballot/candidate-links-2026.json`. **The other 65 have no recorded
site**, so doors 3 and 4 have nothing to point at for them — the missing
rung is a *site* hunt, not a *photo* hunt, and it is named here rather than
counted as a photo failure. Door 4's `.gov` half does not reach them either:
a challenger holds no office, so no government body publishes their portrait.

### The 48 curated local roster members

Still the named remainder, unchanged from round 4. Their rung is door 2 (the
body's own page) and it is unaffected by this ruling.

## Round 3 — New Hampshire's own chambers, and the locals (2026-08-15, D50)

**535 of 750 → 590 of 750.** Sixty-nine portraits shipped: 24 New Hampshire
senators, 25 New Hampshire representatives, and 20 curated local officials.
Every one under door 2 — the portrait a government body publishes to
identify its own member — with the page URL recorded, attribution to the
publishing body, and the same remove-on-request posture. Every one was
looked at before it shipped, in three contact sheets.

Per-surface, against the census's own denominators:

| Surface | Before | After | Remaining initials |
|---|---|---|---|
| state legislators | 59 / 130 | 94 / 130 | 36 |
| curated local rosters | 3 / 71 | 23 / 71 | 48 |
| ballot candidates | 33 / 109 | 33 / 109 | 76 |
| federal officials | 437 / 437 | 437 / 437 | 0 |
| statewide executives | 3 / 3 | 3 / 3 | 0 |

### Three identity joins the hunt could not previously make

The New Hampshire General Court publishes a portrait for nearly every member
and we were shipping 28 of 152. Not one of the three causes was a missing
photo; all three were our confirmation refusing a real one, which is the
right failure direction and still a failure.

**The Senate keys by district, not by member.** The roster links
`.../Senate/members/webpages/district23.aspx`; the portrait on it is
`.../Senate/images/senators/23.jpg`. Neither string carries the senator's
name or any member id, so the generic check rejected all 24. The join is
still structural — we arrive at district 23's page because the General
Court's own member directory says that is Bill Gannon's page, and we accept
the image only because the number in its filename is the district the page
is. Narrowed to the chamber's own senators image path so a stray two-digit
coincidence cannot satisfy it.

**Two senators were recorded as having no portrait, and both did.** Daryl
Abbas and Sharon Carson's pages label BOTH images `alt="NH State Seal"` —
the seal and the senator — so the junk filter threw the portrait away and
the hunt wrote down "page published no image that could be a portrait". A
fabricated absence, caught only because the never-assert-absence rule sent
us to look at the two pages instead of believing the record. An image whose
own file path identifies the member now survives the filter: the site's
prose can be wrong, its filing is what it filed.

**The House names portraits with an id that appears nowhere else.** A member
page is `member.aspx?pid=10678` and its portrait is
`memberpics/409188.jpg` — a different internal id, absent from the URL, and
the alt text reads the generic "member picture" for all 386 of them. What
the page does say is `<img id="pageBody_imgMember">`: the chamber's own
template declaring which element is this member. That declaration is now the
confirmation, and 27 members with a published portrait stopped being misses.

### What is genuinely absent, and what is deferred

Of the 36 state legislators still on initials, **32 are the state's own
`nophoto.jpg`** — the New Hampshire House publishing an absence for its own
member, which for a 400-seat volunteer chamber is an honest end state and is
recorded per member. Three portraits exist but are too small to be portraits
(199×150, 87×152, 98×138 — the banner-shape rejection standing). One member's
roster link is a personal WordPress site, which door 2 does not reach.

Of the 48 local officials still on initials, the rungs are recorded per
person: two bodies 403 a plain client (Newbury's selectboard page and
Kearsarge's school-board page), and the rest publish a roster with no
portrait carrying the member's name, or more than one image naming them —
refused rather than guessed.

**Deferred, and named rather than quietly dropped:** the Commons sweep for
the members the state itself lacks, and for the ~76 ballot challengers still
on initials. This session took the brief's sanctioned cut — NH members
first — and the challenger sweep is the next session's work. Equal effort
per candidate is unaffected: no contest gained a portrait for some
candidates and not others this round, because no ballot candidate gained one
at all.

## Round 2 — the second door (2026-08-14, the tester's mobile pass, D50)

**The rule gained a second acceptable basis** (CLAUDE.md, the author's
decision): a photo ships on (1) an explicit recorded licence, OR (2) an
**official government-published portrait** — the portrait a government
body publishes to identify its own member — with the source URL
recorded, attribution to that body, and a documented remove-on-request
posture (now on the transparency page, shipped in the same commit).
Commercial photography still needs an explicit licence, no exceptions.
The clean-solo-portrait and never-wrong-face standards did not move.
Every manifest row now records **which basis** it ships on (`basis:
"OFFICIAL_PORTRAIT"`, or a licence string).

**476 of 750 → 535 of 750.** Manifest: 721 shipped portraits, of which
**281 under door 2** and 440 under a recorded licence; 410 recorded
misses, every one carrying its rung.

**First, the wrong faces.** Katie Hobbs shipped a Gage Skidmore event
candid (CC BY-SA 3.0, judged last session "solo speaking-event
portrait") — replaced with the state's own studio portrait off
azgovernor.gov. Checking the other two governors while there found both
also wrong in kind: **Kelly Ayotte carried her 112th-Congress U.S.
SENATE portrait** — a different office, roughly fourteen years old — and
Mikie Sherrill a Commons headshot. All three now carry their own
offices' official portraits, each eyeballed. New Hampshire's site 403s a
plain client; the portrait was retrieved on the next rung through a real
browser at the same published URL — a public page, no access control
touched.

**Then the tier the old rule blocked whole.** Every state legislator's
chamber member page was walked, joined by the chamber's own stable id
already in our roster (`scripts/hunt-chamber-portraits.mts`):
**620 attempted, 281 portrait URLs found, 339 recorded misses**; of the
281, **278 staged and shipped**, 3 rejected on shape (banner crops at
87×152, 200×159, 199×150). By chamber: **AZ 90/90, NJ 118/120, NH
73/410**. New Hampshire's number is not our failure — the NH House
serves a literal `nophoto.jpg` for members it publishes no portrait of,
which is an absence on their side and is recorded as one on each row.
The identity join is structural, not a name search: we arrive by the id
we already hold, and the portrait is confirmed by the member's name or
by that same id in the file name; ambiguity refuses. **A 22-face sample
was eyeballed as a contact sheet across all three chambers** (azleg 7,
gc.nh.gov 7, njleg 8) — all clean solo portraits.

Of those 278, **59 land inside the census's 130-legislator universe**
(the legislators actually rendered at the four covered zips); the rest
serve legislators outside current coverage and are banked for when it
grows. The 71 remaining are all New Hampshire.

Rungs NOT reached this session, recorded as open work rather than as
findings: the Commons sweep for the 71 NH legislators without a chamber
portrait, the 68 curated locals, the ~76 ballot challengers, and the
drafted email rung (`docs/photo-requests.md` was not written). D50 stays
**In progress**.

## Round 1 — the dedicated photo session

The dedicated photo session ran 2026-08-14 (branch `claude/photos-0814`):
the before/after is **16 of 747 → 476 of 750** (the denominator grew by
the three governors, who render on every who-governs card and were a
census gap). Tier 1 fetched the GPO pictorial for every renderable
member of Congress by bioguide id — federal reads **437/437**. Tier 2's
chamber terms hunt found NO reuse grant on any of the three legislature
sites (azleg.gov, gc.nh.gov, njleg.state.nj.us — findings recorded per
missing row, FOR COUNSEL), so all 130 state legislators stayed honest
initials **at the time**; the three governors shipped via
attribute-verified Wikimedia Commons portraits instead. (Round 2 above
supersedes both outcomes: door 2 unblocked the chamber tier, and all
three governors' Commons images turned out to be wrong in kind.) Tier 3 walked every ballot candidate and
curated local through the Commons rung (identity by P1157 stable id for
members of Congress, position-held + place verification for everyone
else, ambiguity refused), the campaign og:image rung for all 15 recorded
campaign sites, and the jurisdiction-terms rung for the three largest
local bodies (no reuse grants — recorded). Every staged image was
individually eyeballed before shipping; every miss carries its rungs
with dates. Verification: a 21-member federal sample was cross-checked
against bioguide.congress.gov (an independent archive keyed by the same
stable id — same face on every pair), and a 390×844 walk across all four
zips confirmed 30+ portraits rendering from our origin with honest
initials where photos failed judgment.

## Official portraits — the manifest, `lib/registry/official-photos.json`

Generated from the manifest by `scripts/photo-census.mts` — the table
cannot drift from what ships. All images are served from our origin
(`public/officials/`), metadata-stripped before commit
(`scripts/strip-image-metadata.mts`), and matched by stable id (bioguide
for Congress), never by name search.

## Candidate cards — `data/ballot/candidate-cards-2026.json`

Generated from the cards file by `scripts/photo-census.mts`. Each row is
a shipped photo with the judgment and source recorded on the card itself
(`photoSource` renders as the image's title/alt). External photos were
localized 2026-08-14: committed copies under `public/candidates/`,
metadata-stripped, served from our origin. A candidate not listed renders
initials, and every absence is a recorded hunt in the file's `hunts`
array (128 recorded hunts, rung-by-rung with dates).

## The census — every rendered person, counted by script

Run `pnpm exec tsx scripts/photo-census.mts` to regenerate. Counted
2026-08-19 against the committed data. "Portrait"
means the surface serves an actual judged photo; everything else renders
honest initials. The universe is people the product can actually render:
ballot candidacies and lineup nominees, federal officials attached to
covered divisions, state legislators attached to covered divisions, and
every curated local roster member.
